'There is wind' refers to whatever natural wind from the north, south, and so on, there is in each respective season; this arises from the season. But whatever violent wind there is that can break tree trunks and so on, and also whatever other unseasonal wind there is, this is called wind produced by the power of devas. 'It rains' refers to whatever rain there is in the four months of the rainy season; that arises from the season itself. But whatever excessive rain there is even in the rainy season, and whatever rain there is in the months of Citta and Vesākha, that is called rain produced by the power of devas.

Tatridaṃ vatthu – eko kira vassavalāhakadevaputto talakūṭakavāsi khīṇāsavattherassa santikaṃ gantvā vanditvā aṭṭhāsi. Thero “kosi tva”nti pucchi. “Ahaṃ, bhante, vassavalāhakadevaputto”ti. “Tumhākaṃ kira cittena devo vassatī”ti? “Āma, bhante”ti. “Passitukāmā maya”nti[P 2.0352]. “Temissatha, bhante”ti. “Meghasīsaṃ vā gajjitaṃ vā na paññāyati, kathaṃ temissāmā”ti? “Bhante, amhākaṃcittena devo vassati, tumhe paṇṇasālaṃ pavisathā”ti[V 2.0316]. “Sādhu devaputtā”ti so pāde dhovitvā paṇṇasālaṃ pāvisi. Devaputto tasmiṃ pavisanteyeva ekaṃ gītaṃ gāyitvā hatthaṃ ukkhipi. Samantā tiyojanaṭṭhānaṃ ekameghaṃ ahosi. Thero addhatinto paṇṇasālaṃ paviṭṭhoti. Apica devo nāmesa aṭṭhahi kāraṇehi vassati nāgānubhāvena supaṇṇānubhāvena devatānubhāvena saccakiriyāya utusamuṭṭhānena mārāvaṭṭanena iddhibalena vināsameghenāti.

In this regard, there is this story. It is said that a certain rain-cloud devaputta went to the Arahant Thera residing at Talakūṭaka, paid homage, and stood to one side. The Thera asked, 'Who are you?' 'Venerable sir, I am a rain-cloud devaputta.' 'Is it true that it rains by your will?' 'Yes, venerable sir.' 'We wish to see it.' 'You will get wet, venerable sir.' 'The edge of a cloud or the sound of thunder is not apparent; how shall we get wet?' 'Venerable sir, it rains by our will. Please enter the leaf-hut.' 'Very well, devaputta,' he said, and after washing his feet, he entered the leaf-hut. As the Thera was entering, the devaputta sang a song and raised his hand. All around, over an area of three yojanas, there was a single downpour. The Thera entered the leaf-hut half-drenched. Furthermore, this thing called rain falls for eight reasons: by the power of nāgas, by the power of supaṇṇas, by the power of devas, by an act of truth, by the season, by the turning of Māra, by psychic power, and by the world-dissolution rain.

Valāhakasaṃyuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Valāhaka Saṃyutta is concluded.

12. Vacchagottasaṃyuttavaṇṇanā

12. Commentary on the Vacchagotta Saṃyutta

607-661. Vacchagottasaṃyutte [M 2.0323][V 2.0317] aññāṇāti aññāṇena. Evaṃ sabbapadesu karaṇavaseneva attho veditabbo. Sabbāni cetāni aññamaññavevacanānevāti. Imasmiñca pana saṃyutte ekādasa suttāni pañcapaññāsa veyyākaraṇānīti veditabbāni.

In the Vacchagotta Saṃyutta, the word aññāṇā is to be understood as aññāṇena, 'through ignorance.' The meaning should be understood in this way in all passages, by the power of the instrumental case. Indeed, all these terms are synonyms for one another. Furthermore, in this Saṃyutta, it should be understood that there are eleven suttas and fifty-five expositions.

Vacchagottasaṃyuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Vacchagotta Saṃyutta is concluded.

13. Jhānasaṃyuttaṃ

13. The Jhāna Saṃyutta

1. Samādhimūlakasamāpattisuttavaṇṇanā

1. Commentary on the Samādhimūlakasamāpattisutta

662. Jhānasaṃyuttassa [V 2.0318] paṭhame samādhikusaloti paṭhamaṃ jhānaṃ pañcaṅgikaṃ dutiyaṃ tivaṅgikanti evaṃ aṅgavavatthānakusalo. Na samādhismiṃ samāpattikusaloti cittaṃ hāsetvā kallaṃ katvā jhānaṃ samāpajjituṃ na sakkoti. Iminā nayena sesapadānipi veditabbāni.

662. In the first sutta of the Jhāna Saṃyutta, 'skilled in concentration' means one is skilled in distinguishing the factors, as in: 'the first jhāna is five-factored; the second is three-factored.' 'Not skilled in attaining concentration' means one is not able to enter jhāna after making the mind joyful and ready. By this method, the remaining passages should also be understood.

2-55. Samādhimūlakaṭhitisuttādivaṇṇanā

Commentary on the Samādhimūlakaṭhitisutta and so forth.

663-716. Dutiyādīsu na samādhismiṃ ṭhitikusaloti jhānaṃ ṭhapetuṃ akusalo, sattaṭṭhaaccharāmattaṃ jhānaṃ ṭhapetuṃ na sakkoti. Na samādhismiṃ vuṭṭhānakusaloti jhānato vuṭṭhātuṃ [P 2.0353] akusalo, yathāparicchedena vuṭṭhātuṃ na sakkoti. Na samādhismiṃ kallitakusaloti cittaṃ hāsetvā kallaṃ kātuṃ akusalo. Na samādhismiṃ ārammaṇakusaloti kasiṇārammaṇesu akusalo. Na samādhismiṃ gocarakusaloti kammaṭṭhānagocare ceva bhikkhācāragocare ca akusalo. Na samādhismiṃ abhinīhārakusaloti kammaṭṭhānaṃ abhinīharituṃ akusalo. Na samādhismiṃ sakkaccakārīti jhānaṃ appetuṃ sakkaccakārī na hoti. Na samādhismiṃ sātaccakārīti jhānappanāya satatakārī na hoti, kadācideva karoti. Na samādhismiṃ sappāyakārīti samādhissa sappāye upakārakadhamme pūretuṃ na sakkoti. Tato paraṃ samāpattiādīhi [V 2.0319] padehi [M 2.0324] yojetvā catukkā vuttā. Tesaṃ attho vuttanayeneva veditabbo. Sakalaṃ panettha jhānasaṃyuttaṃ lokiyajjhānavaseneva kathitanti.

663-716. In the second sutta and those that follow, 'unskilled in the stability of concentration' means one is unskilled in establishing jhāna; one is not able to establish jhāna for as long as seven or eight finger-snaps. 'Unskilled in emerging from concentration' means one is unskilled in emerging from jhāna; one is not able to emerge at the predetermined time. 'Unskilled in the readiness of concentration' means one is unskilled in making the mind joyful and ready. 'Unskilled in the object of concentration' means one is unskilled in regard to the kasiṇa objects. 'Unskilled in the domain of concentration' means one is unskilled in both the domain of the meditation subject and the domain of the alms-round. 'Unskilled in the directing of concentration' means one is unskilled in directing the meditation subject. 'Not one who acts carefully in concentration' means one is not one who acts carefully to enter jhāna. 'Not one who acts persistently in concentration' means one is not one who acts constantly for the absorption of jhāna, but does so only occasionally. 'Not one who acts suitably for concentration' means one is not able to fulfill the suitable, supportive qualities for concentration. After that, tetrads are stated, connected with terms such as 'attainment' and so on. Their meaning should be understood in the way already stated. Here, the entire Jhāna Saṃyutta is explained solely by way of the mundane jhānas.

Jhānasaṃyuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Jhāna Saṃyutta is concluded.

Iti sāratthappakāsiniyā saṃyuttanikāya-aṭṭhakathāya

Thus, in the Sāratthappakāsinī, the commentary on the Saṃyutta Nikāya,

Khandhavaggavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Khandhavagga is concluded.

Saṃyuttanikāya-aṭṭhakathāya dutiyo bhāgo.

The Second Part of the Commentary on the Saṃyutta Nikāya.
